<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Exact worst-case reconstruction of a binary word from independent deletion traces requires $`n^{\Omega(\log\log n)}`$ samples for every fixed deletion probability $`q\in(0,1)`$, even with unrestricted computation and any fixed positive success probability. This gives a negative answer to the polynomial-sample question for binary trace reconstruction. More generally, when $`q^3\log n\to\infty`$, we prove a lower bound of $`n^{c\log(q^3\log n)}`$ samples for every fixed $`0\lt c\lt 1/(4\log2)`$. Here <i>q</i> is the known deletion probability, and all logarithms are natural.

- **claim-main-sample-lower-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Fix $s\in(0,1]$ and $0<c<1/(4\log2)$. Along every sequence of instances $(n,q)$ with $n\to\infty$, $q\in(0,1)$, and $q^3\log n\to\infty$, $$\label{eq:main-bound} T_{q,s}(n)\ge n^{c\log(q^3\log n)}$$ eventually. All logarithms are natural. In particular, for each fixed deletion probability $q\in(0,1)$ and fixed positive success probability $s$, the required sample number is $n^{\Omega(\log\log n)}$.
  - Explanation: For every fixed success probability and every admissible constant c, the number of traces needed grows at least like the stated power of n. At a fixed deletion probability this is a superpolynomial sample requirement.
- **claim-superpoly-tv** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-tv)): For every fixed deletion probability $q\in(0,1)$, $$\label{eq:min-tv} \lim_{n\to\infty}n^A \min_{\substack{x,y\in\{0,1\}^n\\x\ne y}} \mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\bigl(\mathcal D_q(x),\mathcal D_q(y)\bigr)=0 \qquad\text{for every }A>0.$$ At this same channel $T_{q,s}(n)/n^A\to\infty$ for every fixed $A>0$ and $s\in(0,1]$.
  - Explanation: At a fixed channel there are two distinct words of the same length whose one-trace laws are closer than any inverse polynomial. The sample complexity therefore outgrows every fixed polynomial.
- **claim-uniform-scale-margins** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-scale-margins)): For the parameters above, $$\label{eq:smallest-scale} \log N_0\gtrsim_B (\log n)2^{-BD} \gtrsim_B (\log n)H_{\rm del}^{-(1-\zeta)} =\Lambda H_{\rm del}^{\zeta}.$$ Moreover, $$\epsilon\log N_0\gtrsim_B \frac{(\log n)^\zeta}{1+\log\log n} \gg\log\log n+\log(d_D+B+1).$$ For every fixed nonnegative integer $h$, uniformly over $0\le i\le D$, $$\label{eq:uniform-absorption} (D+1)^h\Lambda=o(\epsilon\log N_0),\qquad \exp\bigl(O_B((D+1)^h\Lambda)\bigr)=N_i^{o(\epsilon)}.$$
  - Explanation: The smallest scale is large enough that channel factors and accumulated losses over all blocks are absorbed into small powers of each scale.
- **claim-construction-all-scales** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-construction)): Fix $B,\zeta,c_+$ and the parameters of this section. Along every sequence with $n\to\infty$ and $\delta^3\log n\to\infty$, for all sufficiently large instances there exist level-$i$ pairs satisfying Definition <a href="#def:invariant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:invariant">4</a> simultaneously for $0\le i\le D$. The choices of $C,\omega$ depend only on $\delta$ and obey the displayed logarithmic bounds, and one $M_B$ depending only on $B$ works at every level.
  - Explanation: Along the stated regime, pairs of words with auxiliary laws satisfying the level invariant exist simultaneously at every level, with constants that do not change from level to level.
- **claim-multilinear-moment-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-moment)): Let $t\ge2$ be an even integer, let $h\ge0$, and let $(Z_j)_{j\in\mathcal J}$ be a finite family of independent centered real variables with $\\|Z_j\\|_t\le h$. For an integer $r\ge1$, the degree-$r$ multilinear polynomial with real coefficients $A(S)$ on $r$-subsets satisfies $$\label{eq:hc} \left\\|\sum_{\|S\|=r} A(S)\prod_{j\in S}Z_j\right\\|_t \le (C_1 t h)^r \\|A\\|_{\ell^2},$$ where $\ell^2$ uses counting measure and $C_1$ is absolute.
  - Explanation: The norm of a multilinear polynomial in independent centered variables with bounded moments is controlled by the coefficient array in the sum-of-squares norm, without symmetry or identical distribution.
- **claim-lift-adjacent-power-overlap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lift)): Let $w$ be a nonempty word, $k$ a positive integer, and $0<\omega<1/8$ such that $\mathop{\mathrm{ov}}(\mathrm{Tr}(w^k),\mathrm{Tr}(w^{k+1}))\ge\omega$. There is an absolute constant $C_{\rm abs}$ such that, whenever $C\ge C_{\rm abs}/\omega$, $P$ is a sufficiently large positive integer, and the integer $F$ satisfies $F\ge CkP$, we have $$\label{eq:lift} \mathop{\mathrm{ov}}(\mathrm{Tr}(w^{FP}),\mathrm{Tr}(w^{FP+P}))\ge 2/3.$$ It is enough that $P$ exceed an absolute constant, uniformly in $w,k,\omega,C,F$.
  - Explanation: A small overlap between traces of adjacent powers is amplified to a constant overlap at much larger replicated powers.
- **claim-clipping-common-event** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-clipping)): Fix a level $0\le i\le D$ and its parameters from Section <a href="#sec:scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:scales">2</a>. Suppose preliminary oracle laws have common “side” metadata with law $Q_{\rm side}$, and conditional densities $G_x,G_y$ over a conditional reference $Q$, possibly depending on metadata. Require $\int G_x\,dQ=\int G_y\,dQ=1$ for $Q_{\rm side}$-almost every metadata value. Use $Q_{\rm side}Q$ as the joint reference. Suppose on a typical metadata event of probability $\ge1-o(\Delta)$ we have uniform estimates $$\label{eq:clipcond} \\|G_x-1\\|_{r_i},\\|G_y-1\\|_{r_i}\le N_i^{-\rho}, \qquad \\|G_y-G_x\\|_{r_i}^2\le N_i^{-d_i-.01}$$ in $L^{r_i}(Q)$, where $\rho\ge 2^{-4B-10}$. Then conditioning each law on the event consisting of typical metadata and $G_x\ge1/2$ changes it in total variation by $o(\Delta)$. The conditioned laws $\mu,\nu$ satisfy $\nu\ll\mu$ and $$\\|d\nu/d\mu-1\\|_{L^{r_i}(\mu)}^2\le N_i^{-d_i}.$$ All these conclusions hold uniformly over $0\le i\le D$.
  - Explanation: Conditioning both preliminary laws on a common typical event costs little in total variation and yields genuine domination with the required score bound.
- **claim-initial-pair** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-initial)): For the parameters of Section <a href="#sec:scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:scales">2</a>, there is a choice of the common constants in Definition <a href="#def:invariant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:invariant">4</a> with $M_B=1$ for which a level-zero pair exists with simulation error $E_0\le\Delta$. One may choose $\omega=\exp(-C_9\Lambda)$ for an absolute constant $C_9$, and $C>2$ with $\log C=O(\Lambda)$.
  - Explanation: A seed pair of words at the base scale exists with small simulation error, the initial score exponent and an explicit overlap, built from periodic defects with signed shifts.
- **claim-base-rank-kernel-square-sum** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-base-kernel)): For the $u$-period construction and admissible count above, and every $1\le r\le u$, $$\label{eq:abase} \\|A_{r,\mathbf b}\\|_{\ell^2}^2 \le (C_3/\delta)^r(u/m)^{r/2}r^{-r/2},$$ where $\ell^2$ uses counting measure on increasing rank tuples and $C_3$ is absolute, uniformly in the deterministic period types and the admissible count.
  - Explanation: The coefficient arrays of the rank kernel have a square sum that decays geometrically in the degree, uniformly in the period types and admissible counts.
- **claim-base-finite-differences** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-base-differences)): Fix $1\le r<R_0$, an admissible central count, and a tagged tuple in $K$ periods with coarse gaps $\gamma_i$. For disjoint sets $\mathcal D,\mathcal V\subseteq\{1,\ldots,r\}$, put $v'=\|\mathcal D\|+2\|\mathcal V\|$. Then $$\label{eq:abase-diff} \left\\|\prod_{h\in\mathcal D}D_h \prod_{h\in\mathcal V}V_h W_{\mathbf j}\right\\|_{\ell^2}^2 \le \delta^{-O_r(1)}\sqrt{Km}\,m^{-v'} \prod_{i=0}^r(m\gamma_i)^{-1/2}.$$ The implicit constants depend only on $r$.
  - Explanation: Finite differences of a rank kernel gain a factor per difference order, with a bound depending on the coarse gaps of the tagged tuple.
- **claim-base-fixed-period-sums** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-base-fixed-sums)): For fixed $1\le r<R_0$, uniformly in $h$ and $j\in[K/3,2K/3]$, $$\label{eq:base-fixed-sums} \Sigma_{r,h}(j)\le C_r(Km)^{-1/2}\lambda^{r-1},\qquad \Sigma_{r,h}^{\rm bd}(j) \le C_{r,s_0}(Km)^{-1/2}m^{-1/2}\lambda^{r-2}\quad(r\ge2).$$
  - Explanation: Sums over coarse gaps with one period fixed are bounded, with a better bound when some internal gap is short.
- **claim-one-amplification-block** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-block)): For the parameters of Section <a href="#sec:scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:scales">2</a>, let $1\le i\le D$ and suppose a level-$(i-1)$ pair satisfies Definition <a href="#def:invariant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:invariant">4</a> with constants $C,\omega,M_B$. Set $$C'=\max\{C,C_{\rm abs}/\omega\}.$$ The input pair remains valid when $C$ is replaced by $C'$, and there exists a level-$i$ pair satisfying the definition with the same constants $C',\omega,M_B$, with $$d_i=d_{i-1}+(b_i-1)/4-.05, \qquad E_i\le\Delta+(\ell_i/\ell_{i-1})E_{i-1}.$$ Every preceding level constructed with $C$ also remains valid with $C'$, and this enlarged value is retained in later blocks. The conclusion is uniform over all levels along the stated joint asymptotic regime.
  - Explanation: From a valid pair at one level one builds a valid pair at the next level, with the score exponent raised by the stated increment and the simulation error recursion.
- **claim-signed-binomial-smoothing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-binomial-smoothing)): Let $\sigma$ be the Prouhet–Thue–Morse sequence of order $s_*$ above. Suppose $\pi$ is an integer translation or reflection of a fair binomial probability mass function with $g\ge c_0\omega^2m$ trials, where $c_0>0$ is absolute and $m\to\infty$ as in the construction. Then $$\begin{aligned} \\|\sigma*\pi\\|_\infty &\le \exp(O((s_*+1)^2+(s_*+1)\Lambda))\, m^{-(s_*+1)/2},\nonumber\\ \\|\sigma*\pi\\|_2^2 &\le \exp(O((s_*+1)^2+(s_*+1)\Lambda))\, m^{-s_*-1/2},\qquad \\|\pi\\|_\infty\le O(\omega^{-1}m^{-1/2}).\label{eq:sig_conv} \end{aligned}$$ The norms use counting measure on the integers, and the implicit constants may depend on $c_0$.
  - Explanation: Convolving the signed Prouhet–Thue–Morse sequence with a broad fair binomial offset gives sup-norm and square-sum bounds that decay as a high power of the number of trials.
- **claim-nested-atom-overlap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-nested-overlap)): Under the input hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:block" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:block">13</a>, the words and counts constructed above satisfy, for every $1\le j\le b$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{ov}}\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}((U_j^+)^{h_j}),\mathrm{Tr}((U_j^+)^{h_j+1})\bigr)\ge\omega.$$
  - Explanation: Adjacent powers of every nested atom keep trace overlap at least the common threshold, proved by induction on depth with Hellinger replacement costs.
- **claim-tree-simulation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tree-simulation)): For the unconditioned surrogate laws and the common projection above, $$\label{eq:projection_tree} \begin{aligned} \mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathcal K_{\rm tree}\widetilde\mu_i,\mathrm{Tr}(U_b^+)) &\le o(\Delta)+\left(\prod_{j=1}^bT_j\right)E_{i-1},\\ \mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathcal K_{\rm tree}\widetilde\nu_i,\mathrm{Tr}(U_b^-)) &\le o(\Delta)+\left(\prod_{j=1}^bT_j\right)E_{i-1}. \end{aligned}$$ All error terms are uniform in $i$.
  - Explanation: The common projection of the auxiliary tree observation reproduces a genuine trace up to a small error, plus the inherited per-leaf errors.
- **claim-tree-score-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tree-score)): For every exact typical metadata value $\mathfrak d$, the densities in <a href="#eq:conditional-densities" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:conditional-densities">[eq:conditional-densities]</a> satisfy $$\\|G_+-G_-\\|_{L^{r_i}(Q)}^2\le N^{-d'-.01}.$$ The bound is uniform in $\mathfrak d$ and the block level $i$.
  - Explanation: For every typical report, the two conditional densities differ by a squared norm smaller than the next-level score target, which is the gain obtained from the forced binary subtree.
- **claim-restoration-padding** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-restoration)): For the parameters in Section <a href="#sec:scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:scales">2</a>, there are distinct words $x,y\in\{0,1\}^n$ such that, for every positive integer $m$, $$\label{eq:restored-product} \mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\bigl(\mathcal D_q(x)^{\otimes m},\mathcal D_q(y)^{\otimes m}\bigr) \le \sqrt{m n^{-d_D}}+2mn(D+1)\Delta.$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:restored-single} \mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\bigl(\mathcal D_q(x),\mathcal D_q(y)\bigr) \le n^{-d_D/2}+2n(D+1)\Delta.$$
  - Explanation: After padding, there are distinct words of length n whose multi-trace laws at the requested channel are close in total variation by an explicit bound.

      "exact_statement": "Fix a level $0\\le i\\le D$ and its parameters from Section <a href=\"#sec:scales\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:scales\">2</a>. Suppose preliminary oracle laws have common “side” metadata with law $Q_{\\rm side}$, and conditional densities $G_x,G_y$ over a conditional reference $Q$, possibly depending on metadata. Require $\\int G_x\\,dQ=\\int G_y\\,dQ=1$ for $Q_{\\rm side}$-almost every metadata value. Use $Q_{\\rm side}Q$ as the joint reference. Suppose on a typical metadata event of probability $\\ge1-o(\\Delta)$ we have uniform estimates $$\\label{eq:clipcond}\n\\|G_x-1\\|_{r_i},\\|G_y-1\\|_{r_i}\\le N_i^{-\\rho},\n\\qquad \\|G_y-G_x\\|_{r_i}^2\\le N_i^{-d_i-.01}$$ in $L^{r_i}(Q)$, where $\\rho\\ge 2^{-4B-10}$. Then conditioning each law on the event consisting of typical metadata and $G_x\\ge1/2$ changes it in total variation by $o(\\Delta)$. The conditioned laws $\\mu,\\nu$ satisfy $\\nu\\ll\\mu$ and $$\\|d\\nu/d\\mu-1\\|_{L^{r_i}(\\mu)}^2\\le N_i^{-d_i}.$$ All these conclusions hold uniformly over $0\\le i\\le D$.",
      "explanation": "Conditioning both preliminary laws on a common typical event costs little in total variation and yields genuine domination with the required score bound.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\le r<R_0$, an admissible central count, and a tagged tuple in $K$ periods with coarse gaps $\\gamma_i$.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\sigma$ be the Prouhet–Thue–Morse sequence of order $s_*$ above. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\sigma*\\pi\\|_\\infty\n &\\le \\exp(O((s_*+1)^2+(s_*+1)\\Lambda))\\,\n       m^{-(s_*+1)/2},\\nonumber\\\\\n \\|\\sigma*\\pi\\|_2^2\n &\\le \\exp(O((s_*+1)^2+(s_*+1)\\Lambda))\\,\n       m^{-s_*-1/2},\\qquad\n \\|\\pi\\|_\\infty\\le O(\\omega^{-1}m^{-1/2}).\\label{eq:sig_conv}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The norms use counting measure on the integers, and the implicit constants may depend on $c_0$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **deletion trace law** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a binary word $x$ and a known deletion probability $q\in(0,1)$, let $\mathcal D_q(x)$ denote the law obtained by deleting each bit independently with probability $q$ and concatenating the surviving bits in their original order.
- **sample complexity** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For $s\in(0,1]$, let $T_{q,s}(n)$ be the least nonnegative integer $m$ for which an estimator, given $n,q$ and $m$ independent complete traces, recovers every $x\in\{0,1\}^n$ with probability at least $s$.
- **overlap** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For probability laws $P,Q$, define overlap by $\mathop{\mathrm{ov}}(P,Q)=1-\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(P,Q)$, the largest mass of a common subprobability.
- **squared Hellinger distance** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Our squared Hellinger distance is $$H^2(P,Q)=\int(\sqrt{dP}-\sqrt{dQ})^2.$$
- **working deletion parameters** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For the rest of the proof put $$\delta=\min(q,1/2),\qquad \Lambda=\delta^{-3},\qquad H_{\rm del}=\delta^3\log n.$$
- **level invariant** ([source](#stmt-def-invariant)): Choose $C>2$ and $0<\omega<1/8$ depending only on $\delta$, with $$\log C=O(\Lambda),\qquad \log(\omega^{-1})=O(\Lambda),$$ and a constant $M_B\ge0$ depending only on $B$. The same choices are used at every level. For $0\le i\le D$, a level-$i$ pair consists of two different strings $x_i,y_i$ of the same length $\ell_i\in[N_i/2,N_i]$, and probability laws $\mu_i,\nu_i$ on a common auxiliary space, which may depend on $n$ and $\delta$, satisfying the following conditions. - There are a Markov kernel $\mathcal K_i$ from the auxiliary space to finite binary words and a number $E_i\ge0$ such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathcal K_i\mu_i,\mathrm{Tr}(x_i))\le E_i,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathcal K_i\nu_i,\mathrm{Tr}(y_i))\le E_i, \qquad \frac{E_i}{\ell_i}\le(i+1)\Delta.$$ - We have $\nu_i\ll\mu_i$ and $$\left\\|\frac{d\nu_i}{d\mu_i}-1\right\\|_{L^{r_i}(\mu_i)}^2 \le N_i^{-d_i}.$$ - For an integer $k_i\in[\ell_i,C^{2Bi+M_B}\ell_i]$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{ov}}\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}(x_i^{k_i}),\mathrm{Tr}(x_i^{k_i+1})\bigr)\ge\omega.$$ Here powers concatenate copies of the word.
- **ordinary and defect blocks** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For this construction only, let the ordinary block be $\mathsf A=01$ and the defect block be $\mathsf D=1$.
- **silent erasure and marks** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For each block independently, silently erase it with probability $r_e$; otherwise report a mark with law $\nu_H$.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-main-lower-bound | sample complexity lower bound | ≥ | $n^{c\log(q^3\log n)}$ | q^3 log n tends to infinity |
| q-single-trace-tv | single-trace total variation of the padded pair | ≤ | $n^{-d_D/2}+2n(D+1)\Delta$ | parameters of Section 2 |
| q-multi-trace-tv | m-trace total variation of the padded pair | ≤ | $\sqrt{m n^{-d_D}}+2mn(D+1)\Delta$ | parameters of Section 2 |
| q-working-delta | working deletion probability | = | $\min(q,1/2)$ | all instances |
| q-lambda | channel factor Lambda | = | $\delta^{-3}$ | all instances |
| q-h-del | depth scale H_del | = | $\delta^3\log n$ | all instances |
| q-epsilon | scale margin epsilon | = | $2^{-4B-100}/(D+1)$ | fixed B |
| q-preliminary-blocks | number of preliminary blocks | = | $5\cdot 2^B$ | fixed B |
| q-initial-score-exponent | initial score exponent | = | $2.20$ | seed pair |
| q-score-exponent-step | score exponent recursion | = | $d_{i-1}+(b_i-1)/4-.05$ | 1 ≤ i ≤ D |
| q-error-tolerance | error tolerance Delta | = | $n^{-100(d_D+B+1)}$ | fixed B |
| q-lift-overlap | amplified adjacent-power overlap | ≥ | $2/3$ | F large relative to kP |
| q-tree-score-bound | squared conditional score difference | ≤ | $N^{-d'-.01}$ | uniform in block level |
| q-seed-eta | seed exponent margin eta | = | $.004$ | level zero |
| q-seed-pattern-order | seed cancellation order | = | $300$ | level zero |
| q-degree-cutoff | seed degree cutoff | = | $3000$ | level zero |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-scale-margins** [claim](#stmt-lem-scale-margins) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scale-margins): Multiply contraction bounds over the blocks and compare with the definition of the depth. -> smallest scale and absorption of channel factors
- **step-moment** [claim](#stmt-lem-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-moment): Scalar moment estimate, then iteration over coordinates by conditioning. -> multilinear moment bound
- **step-lift** [claim](#stmt-lem-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lift): Random chunk schedule with hidden flags and a hypergeometric count comparison. -> constant overlap at large powers
- **step-clipping** [claim](#stmt-lem-clipping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-clipping): Condition on a common typical event and compare likelihood ratios in high moments. -> domination and score bound for conditioned laws
- **step-base-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-base-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-base-kernel): Inner products of rank kernels, binomial convolution bound, lattice sums over coarse gaps. -> square-sum bound for the coefficient arrays
- **step-base-differences** [claim](#stmt-lem-base-differences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-base-differences): Express shifts through gap-length differences and use Fourier bounds for binomial differences. -> difference bound
- **step-base-fixed-sums** [claim](#stmt-lem-base-fixed-sums) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-base-fixed-sums): Split gaps on each side of the fixed period and sum the free gaps. -> gap sum bounds
- **step-initial-pair** [claim](#stmt-prop-initial) / [proof](#pm-h-20): Preliminary observation, signed pattern smoothing, difference estimates, clipping and lifting of the pure-run overlap. -> level-zero pair
- **step-binomial-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-lem-binomial-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-binomial-smoothing): Fourier inversion and Parseval with the signed transform and the binomial transform. -> smoothing bounds
- **step-nested-overlap** [claim](#stmt-lem-nested-overlap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nested-overlap): Induction on depth: lift overlap, then bound Hellinger replacement costs using the old score and signed smoothing. -> overlap of adjacent powers of nested atoms
- **step-tree-simulation** [claim](#stmt-lem-tree-simulation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tree-simulation): Hoeffding bound on displacements, union bound over vertices, coupling on the fit event. -> projection error bound
- **step-tree-score** [claim](#stmt-lem-tree-score) / [proof](#pm-h-34): High-degree truncation, parity expansion, singleton smoothing, forced binary subtree and anchor factorization. -> conditional score gain
- **step-block** [claim](#stmt-prop-block) / [proof](#pm-h-36): Combine overlap, simulation and score gain after clipping and track the error recursion. -> level-i pair
- **step-construction** [claim](#stmt-prop-construction) / [proof](#sec:scales): Induction starting at the initial pair and iterating the block. -> pairs at all levels
- **step-restoration** [claim](#stmt-lem-restoration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restoration): Pad with a common tail, tensorize the Hellinger bound and telescope projection errors. -> multi-trace and single-trace total variation bounds
- **step-scale-use-tv** [claim](#stmt-thm-tv) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restoration-2): Fix parameters independently of A and multiply the single-trace bound by a power of n. -> vanishing limit
- **step-two-point** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-38): Two-point testing bound with a strict exponent margin. -> success at most two thirds below the threshold
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restoration-3): Independent gadgets with a product prior make the success probabilities multiply. -> lower bound for every fixed success probability

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-ptm-sign-cancellation**: The signs used in the seed construction are the coefficients of a product of dyadic factors. They alternate by the parity of the binary digit count of the index, and their generating polynomial vanishes to high order at 1, so every low-order moment of the sign sequence is zero.
- **inv-fixed-channel-superpolynomial-tv**: At every fixed deletion probability, the minimal one-trace total variation distance between distinct words of length n decays faster than any inverse polynomial.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-vanishing-deletion-rate**: Quoting the superpolynomial sample lower bound for channels whose deletion probability shrinks quickly with n. -> The lower bound is proved only along instances where q^3 log n grows without bound, so it says nothing outside that regime.
- **anti-sign-sequence-as-hard-instance**: Treating the executable sign-sequence check as a certificate that a hard pair of words exists at a given length. -> The check only verifies the sign-cancellation ingredient for small orders. The hard pair needs the full multi-scale construction with its auxiliary laws and error budgets, which is not executed.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The paper's main theorems are asymptotic existence statements, but the seed construction uses an explicit finite object: the signed pattern given by the coefficients of a product of dyadic factors. Panel 3 executes only that sub-construction for small orders.

```python
# INPUTS: one integer s >= 0 on stdin, the order of the dyadic sign product.
# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= s <= 16 so the output stays small.
# OUTPUTS: the 2^s integer coefficients of the product of (1 - z^(2^v)) for v below s, space separated, lowest degree first.
# TIME: O(4^s) integer operations by repeated polynomial multiplication.
# SPACE: O(2^s) integers.
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers, no rounding.
# FAILURE: non-integer input raises an exception and exits nonzero.
import sys

s = int(sys.stdin.read().split()[0])
poly = [1]
for v in range(s):
    step = 1 << v
    new = [0] * (len(poly) + step)
    for i, c in enumerate(poly):
        new[i] += c
        new[i + step] -= c
    poly = new
print(" ".join(str(c) for c in poly))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves lower bounds on sample counts and gives no procedure that a program could execute. The one construction is a proof device, and it is defined with unbounded parameters that are not meant to be run. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Hard word pairs are built only inside the proof, with a working deletion rate held fixed across all scales. The paper gives no simulation model, and a game or simulator would gain nothing from it. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is an impossibility statement about estimators. Nothing in it can be packaged as a tool, and the hypotheses concern exact worst-case recovery. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a lower bound on the number of samples, so it constrains every algorithm and exhibits none. The paper explicitly leaves a gap to the known upper bounds and does not supply a reconstruction algorithm. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The induction carries pairs of words with auxiliary laws and kernels. These are analytic bookkeeping objects, not a data structure with operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The model allows unrestricted computation, so the bound is independent of any hardware. It is a statement about information in samples and cannot be improved by faster or parallel machines. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The one-trace indistinguishability result is statistical and concerns a specific deletion channel. It defines no scheme, adversary model or security reduction, so it gives no cryptographic guarantee by itself. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper concerns exact worst-case recovery of one deterministic word, not learning from a data distribution. It has no hypothesis class, loss or generalization claim. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or solver appears. The paper tracks error budgets in an induction, which is a proof requirement and not an optimisation model. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Traces here are independent samples of one channel, with no nodes, messages or fault model. The paper contrasts its setting with lower bounds that restrict the available statistics, which is a different question. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The remaining value is as a theoretical benchmark for information theory: it fixes the sample cost of worst-case deletion channels. It supports no construction, and the regime is narrow. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-regime-growth** (hypothesis): The sample lower bound is proved only along instances where the cube of the deletion probability times the log of the length grows without bound, with the constant c below the stated threshold. Outside this regime the theorem gives no information. Blocked use: ['Applying the superpolynomial bound to deletion probabilities that shrink quickly with n', 'Using a constant c at or above the stated threshold']
- **limit-worst-case-words** (hypothesis): The bounds concern exact recovery of every word of length n from independent complete traces. They are worst-case over deterministic words and do not describe typical or random inputs. Blocked use: ['Claiming that reconstructing random or typical strings needs superpolynomial samples', 'Applying the bound to channels with insertions, substitutions or indexed positions']
- **limit-nonconstructive-lower-bound** (nonconstructive): The results are impossibility statements proved by two-point testing between a constructed pair of words. They exhibit no reconstruction algorithm and leave a gap to the known upper bounds. Blocked use: ['Reading the theorem as a procedure to implement', 'Treating the bound as tight in the exponent']
- **limit-level-invariant-constants** (formal_scope): The induction needs constants chosen depending only on the working deletion rate, with logarithmic bounds in terms of the channel factor, and one count constant depending only on the block depth. Results hold eventually, for sufficiently large instances. Blocked use: ['Using the construction at small n with explicit constants', 'Assuming the constants are independent of the channel']
- **limit-seed-stage** (approximation): The seed stage uses a fixed degree cutoff and a fixed cancellation order, and every estimate is asymptotic in the base scale, so it supplies no explicit small-size instances. Blocked use: ['Extracting concrete hard word pairs for small lengths from the seed alone']
- **limit-gadget-success** (edge_case): Extending to arbitrary fixed positive success uses several independent gadgets and a product prior; the sample budget zero case is handled separately. Success is fixed while the length grows. Blocked use: ['Letting the target success probability depend on n without redoing the gadget count']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 122 (`lean/docs/122.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Quantitative lower bounds for trace reconstruction](../../preprints/quantitative-lower-bounds-for-trace-reconstruction-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves superpolynomial sample lower bounds for exact worst-case reconstruction of binary words from independent deletion traces, with unrestricted computation and any fixed positive success probability. For every fixed deletion probability $q\in(0,1)$, sample complexity grows faster than every fixed power of the word length; the minimum total-variation distance between two one-trace laws decays faster than every inverse power.
>
> More quantitatively, along sequences with $q^3\log n\to\infty$, the required number of samples is at least $n^{c\log(q^3\log n)}$ eventually for every fixed $0<c<1/(4\log2)$. All logarithms are natural.

Comparator source `TraceReconstruction.lean` (3451 bytes, sha256 `68df708f0b20b524cbc8fd02164731245982749eba0b2e8e0e1a520c85b0e70d`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open scoped ENNReal
noncomputable section

namespace TraceReconstruction

abbrev Word (n : ℕ) := Fin n → Bool
abbrev Mask (n : ℕ) := Fin n → Bool
abbrev Trace := List Bool

/-- `true` retains the bit. The observer gets only the resulting list. -/
def traceOfMask {n : ℕ} (x : Word n) (a : Mask n) : Trace :=
  ((List.finRange n).filter fun i => a i).map x

/-- The deletion-mask probability, with deletion probability `q`. -/
def maskWeight {n : ℕ} (q : ℝ) (a : Mask n) : ℝ :=
  ∏ i, if a i then 1 - q else q

/-- Law of a single complete trace. -/
def traceMass {n : ℕ} (q : ℝ) (x : Word n) (w : Trace) : ℝ :=
  ∑ a : Mask n, if traceOfMask x a = w then maskWeight q a else 0

/-- All possible complete outputs from length-n inputs, with lengths included. -/
def traceSpace (n : ℕ) : Finset Trace :=
  Finset.univ.biUnion fun k : Fin (n + 1) =>
    Finset.univ.image fun x : Word k.val => List.ofFn x

/-- 1/2 sum of absolute differences, on the full finite output space. -/
def traceTV {n : ℕ} (q : ℝ) (x y : Word n) : ℝ :=
  (1 / 2 : ℝ) * ∑ w ∈ traceSpace n, |traceMass q x w - traceMass q y w|

/-- The estimator may depend arbitrarily on n, q and every complete sample.
    Its value is its conditional probability of reporting the given input. -/
def IsEstimator {n m : ℕ} (A : (Fin m → Trace) → Word n → ℝ) : Prop :=
  (∀ z x, 0 ≤ A z x) ∧ (∀ z, ∑ x : Word n, A z x = 1)

/-- Independent masks generate independent complete traces; no retained
    positions, boundaries or other latent variables are revealed to A. -/
def reconstructionSuccess {n m : ℕ} (q : ℝ)
    (A : (Fin m → Trace) → Word n → ℝ) (x : Word n) : ℝ :=
  ∑ a : Fin m → Mask n,
    (∏ j, maskWeight q (a j)) * A (fun j => traceOfMask x (a j)) x

def SufficientBudget (n : ℕ) (q s : ℝ) (m : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ A : (Fin m → Trace) → Word n → ℝ,
    IsEstimator A ∧ ∀ x, s ≤ reconstructionSuccess q A x

/-- Natural fixed sample budget; infimum of the empty set is infinity. -/
noncomputable def sampleComplexity (n : ℕ) (q s : ℝ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  sInf {t | ∃ m : ℕ, SufficientBudget n q s m ∧ t = (m : ℝ≥0∞)}

/-- The real infimum is a minimum for n>0 (a nonempty finite set). -/
noncomputable def minTraceTV (n : ℕ) (q : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  sInf {t | ∃ x y : Word n, x ≠ y ∧ t = traceTV q x y}

open Filter
open scoped Topology Classical ENNReal

theorem quantitative_sample_lower_bound
    (s c : ℝ) (hs : s ∈ Set.Ioc 0 1)
    (hc : c ∈ Set.Ioo 0 (1 / (4 * Real.log 2)))
    (n : ℕ → ℕ) (q : ℕ → ℝ)
    (hn : Tendsto n atTop atTop)
    (hq : ∀ k, q k ∈ Set.Ioo 0 1)
    (hscale : Tendsto (fun k => (q k) ^ 3 * Real.log (n k)) atTop atTop) :
    ∀ᶠ k in atTop,
      ENNReal.ofReal ((n k : ℝ) ^ (c * Real.log ((q k) ^ 3 * Real.log (n k)))) ≤
        sampleComplexity (n k) (q k) s := by
  sorry

theorem superpolynomial_one_trace
    (q : ℝ) (hq : q ∈ Set.Ioo 0 1) (C : ℝ) (hC : 0 < C) :
    Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => (n : ℝ) ^ C * minTraceTV n q) atTop (𝓝 0) := by
  sorry

theorem superpolynomial_sample_complexity
    (q : ℝ) (hq : q ∈ Set.Ioo 0 1) (s : ℝ) (hs : s ∈ Set.Ioc 0 1)
    (C : ℝ) (hC : 0 < C) :
    Tendsto
      (fun n : ℕ => sampleComplexity n q s / ENNReal.ofReal ((n : ℝ) ^ C))
      atTop (𝓝 ⊤) := by
  sorry

end TraceReconstruction

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Exact worst-case reconstruction of a binary word from independent deletion traces requires $`n^{\Omega(\log\log n)}`$ samples for every fixed deletion probability $`q\in(0,1)`$, even with unrestricted computation and any fixed positive success probability. This gives a negative answer to the polynomial-sample question for binary trace reconstruction. More generally, when $`q^3\log n\to\infty`$, we prove a lower bound of $`n^{c\log(q^3\log n)}`$ samples for every fixed $`0\lt c\lt 1/(4\log2)`$. Here <i>q</i> is the known deletion probability, and all logarithms are natural.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For a binary word $x$ and a known deletion probability $q\in(0,1)$, let $\mathcal D_q(x)$ denote the law obtained by deleting each bit independently with probability $q$ and concatenating the surviving bits in their original order. The retention probability is $p=1-q$. A sample from this law is a *complete trace*: its length and all surviving bits are observed, without the positions they occupied in $x$.

For $s\in(0,1]$, let $T_{q,s}(n)$ be the least nonnegative integer $m$ for which an estimator, given $n,q$ and $m$ independent complete traces, recovers every $x\in\{0,1\}^n$ with probability at least $s$. The estimator may be randomized and its computation is unrestricted. If no finite $m$ suffices, set $T_{q,s}(n)=+\infty$. Write $T_q(n)=T_{q,2/3}(n)$. Our lower bounds concern this fixed sample budget.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:main-bound"></a>
**Theorem 1** (Quantitative sample lower bound). *Fix $s\in(0,1]$ and $0<c<1/(4\log2)$. Along every sequence of instances $(n,q)$ with $n\to\infty$, $q\in(0,1)$, and $q^3\log n\to\infty$, $$\label{eq:main-bound}
 T_{q,s}(n)\ge n^{c\log(q^3\log n)}$$ eventually. All logarithms are natural. In particular, for each fixed deletion probability $q\in(0,1)$ and fixed positive success probability $s$, the required sample number is $n^{\Omega(\log\log n)}$.*

</div>

The varying-channel statement keeps the requested channel fixed throughout the construction for each instance; it is allowed to change only when the instance changes. The result gives a negative answer to the polynomial-sample question for deterministic binary inputs at every fixed deletion probability. A separate consequence concerns one-trace indistinguishability itself. For laws on the countable set of finite binary words, write $$\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(P,Q)=\frac12\sum_w|P(w)-Q(w)|.$$

<a id="stmt-thm-tv"></a>
<div id="thm:tv" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:min-tv"></a>
**Theorem 2** (Superpolynomial one-trace indistinguishability). *For every fixed deletion probability $q\in(0,1)$, $$\label{eq:min-tv}
 \lim_{n\to\infty}n^A
 \min_{\substack{x,y\in\{0,1\}^n\\x\ne y}}
 \mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\bigl(\mathcal D_q(x),\mathcal D_q(y)\bigr)=0
 \qquad\text{for every }A>0.$$ At this same channel $T_{q,s}(n)/n^A\to\infty$ for every fixed $A>0$ and $s\in(0,1]$.*

</div>

The channel is fixed independently of $A$. The proof of <a href="#eq:min-tv" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-tv">[eq:min-tv]</a> uses an explicit score and approximation bound. Since the minimum is attained at every $n$, a single sequence of minimizing pairs realizes all the limits simultaneously.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and comparison with earlier results

Reconstructing a sequence from repeated corrupted observations has a long history. Levenshtein studied both combinatorial and memoryless channel formulations (Levenshtein 2001) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Levenshtein2001](#ref-Levenshtein2001)]. Batu, Kannan, Khanna, and McGregor studied the independent-deletion trace problem in connection with multiple sequence alignment and outlined a linear sample obstruction at fixed deletion probability (Batu et al. 2004, sec. 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Batu2004](#ref-Batu2004)]. Later lower bounds for complete traces focused on the difficulty of locating a small discrepancy within a long background. McGregor, Price, and Vorotnikova analyzed neighboring positions of a single one among zeros, using Hellinger distance and common mass (McGregor, Price, and Vorotnikova 2014) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[MPV2014](#ref-MPV2014)]. Holden and Lyons used a defect in an alternating background to prove $\Omega(n^{5/4}/\sqrt{\log n})$ (Holden and Lyons 2020, 2022) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[HoldenLyons2020](#ref-HoldenLyons2020), [HoldenLyonsErratum2022](#ref-HoldenLyonsErratum2022)], and Chase obtained $\Omega(n^{3/2}/\log^7 n)$ by a direct Hellinger calculation for a related pair (Chase 2021a) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Chase2021Lower](#ref-Chase2021Lower)]. The displayed Holden–Lyons and Chase rates fix the channel and a reconstruction success greater than $1/2$. Our seed starts from the periodic defect family used by Chase and makes signed choices across many periods; it also supplies the overlap and score estimates needed for repeated amplification.

For fixed deletion probability, Holenstein, Mitzenmacher, Panigrahy, and Wieder obtained a worst-case upper bound of $\exp(\widetilde O(\sqrt n))$ (Holenstein et al. 2008) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[HMPW2008](#ref-HMPW2008)], where $\widetilde O$ suppresses polylogarithmic factors. Independently, De, O’Donnell, and Servedio and Nazarov and Peres improved it to $\exp(O(n^{1/3}))$ (De, O’Donnell, and Servedio 2017; Nazarov and Peres 2017) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[DOS2017](#ref-DOS2017), [NP2017](#ref-NP2017)], and Chase obtained $\exp(O(n^{1/5}\log^5 n))$ (Chase 2021b) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Chase2021Upper](#ref-Chase2021Upper)]. The 2026 preprint of Burudgunte, Valiant, and Wang gives $\exp(p^{-7/3}(\log_2 n)^{C_0})$ samples for a universal constant $C_0$ and every positive retention probability $p=1-q$ (Burudgunte, Valiant, and Wang 2026) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BVW2026](#ref-BVW2026)]. At fixed $p$ this is a quasipolynomial sample upper bound; the cited preprint does not give a quasipolynomial-time reconstruction algorithm. The logarithm of this sample bound is a fixed power of $\log n$, whereas the logarithm of our lower bound at a fixed channel has order $(\log n)(\log\log n)$, leaving a gap between the rates. The companion paper *Uniform quasipolynomial-time trace reconstruction* proves such a time bound for every fixed known rational retention probability (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[OAI:Uniform-quasipolynomial-time-trace-reconstruction-October-5-2026](#ref-OAI:Uniform-quasipolynomial-time-trace-reconstruction-October-5-2026)].

Polynomial reconstruction remains possible under other channel or input conditions. For every fixed $\varepsilon>0$, Chen, De, Lee, Servedio, and Sinha give reconstruction in polynomial time when $q\le n^{-1/3-\varepsilon}$, using $O(n^{4/3})$ traces (Chen et al. 2021) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[CDLSS2021LowDeletion](#ref-CDLSS2021LowDeletion)]. Those deletion probabilities lie outside the regime $q^3\log n\to\infty$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Aamand, Liu, and Narayanan prove polynomial reconstruction when the internal zero gaps between successive ones have length at least a sufficiently large constant times $(\log n)^8$ and the deletion probability is at most a sufficiently small universal constant (Aamand, Liu, and Narayanan 2025) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ALN2025](#ref-ALN2025)].

Some lower bounds restrict the available statistics. De, O’Donnell, and Servedio measure the precision required of an oracle returning an $\ell^1$ approximation to the expected trace vector padded with zeros, while Nazarov and Peres study repeated observations of one fixed output coordinate (De, O’Donnell, and Servedio 2017; Nazarov and Peres 2017) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[DOS2017](#ref-DOS2017), [NP2017](#ref-NP2017)]. Chen, De, Lee, and Servedio study a tolerance oracle for functions of contiguous blocks of trace coordinates (Chen et al. 2024) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[CDLS2024](#ref-CDLS2024)]. Their lower bounds concern those specified statistics. A different input model is studied by Rivkin, Valiant, and Valiant: the input is a fixed probability vector, with fresh Bernoulli bits drawn from it before each trace. For deletion probability at least a constant times $n^{-1/2}$, their generalized trace laws can have total variation $\exp(-\Omega(\sqrt n))$ (Rivkin, Valiant, and Valiant 2025) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[RivkinValiantValiant2025](#ref-RivkinValiantValiant2025)]. Their arguments using translated segments and cancellation are related to the observation constructed in Section <a href="#sec:layout" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:layout">6</a>. Theorems <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> and <a href="#thm:tv" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:tv">2</a> concern the entire trace law of one deterministic binary word.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Mechanism and proof structure

Our construction hides differences in location at several scales. The basic source of hidden randomness is exact common mass. If trace laws for $k$ and $k+1$ copies of a word share mass $\omega$, place such a pair of runs on opposite sides of a central segment and choose their orientation by a fair bit. Their joint trace laws share a product component of mass $\omega^2$. On that component the orientation remains fair even after the side outputs are reported. Many independent pairs therefore leave a binomial uncertainty in the position of the central segment. Marginalizing the flags restores the product of the two side trace laws for each orientation. Concatenating independent genuine traces of a partition of the fixed word then recovers its trace law exactly. At later scales the preliminary observation uses earlier auxiliary outputs at its leaves and allows rare failed layouts; at every scale, conditioning to obtain likelihood domination adds a small change. The resulting auxiliary laws therefore have an approximate common projection to genuine traces, with all these losses recorded in the invariant.

The two words use complementary signed patterns in their central segments. The generating polynomial of a Prouhet–Thue–Morse pattern vanishes to high order at $1$ (Allouche and Shallit 1999, Proposition 2 and Theorem 6) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[AlloucheShallit1999](#ref-AlloucheShallit1999)], making its convolution with a broad binomial offset small. The same dyadic sign recursion is used by Holenstein et al. (2008, sec. 3.2, equation (3.10)) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[HMPW2008](#ref-HMPW2008)] to produce iterated finite differences of a kernel for coordinate means. In the conditional likelihood difference, each selected leaf set indexes a product of old likelihood scores, one per leaf, multiplied by a coefficient. We expand this coefficient along the tree of nested segments as a sum of expected products of signed motif factors and support indicators, averaging over the hidden bits. Within one product, a child is active at a vertex when it supplies one of these local factors, and the term is odd there when an odd number of the local factors are signed. The complementary words give root types $+1$ and $-1$, so subtracting the two root evaluations cancels every even root term. At a vertex where an odd term has only one active child, the hidden offset smooths its single local signed factor. For an odd root term with no such singleton, selecting two active children recursively produces a forced binary subtree with $2^b$ selected leaves at depth $b$, and hence at least $2^b$ factors from the old score. A shared displacement for each sibling group, together with independent local displacements for its members, spreads these descendant locations. A joint bound that retains the shared displacement gives the extra decay beyond the factors from the old score.

The argument has three quantitative requirements. The initial pair must work uniformly as $q$ decreases. The number of amplification blocks grows with $n$, so constants depending on the channel or cancellation order must remain below the available scale margins. Finally, every oracle conditioning and failed random layout must be charged to an error that remains negligible after taking many traces.

Section <a href="#sec:scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:scales">2</a> states the oracle invariant and the global schedule with two phases. Section <a href="#sec:tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tools">3</a> proves the common-mass, moment, and clipping tools, and Section <a href="#sec:initial" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:initial">4</a> constructs the seed at the working channel. Section <a href="#sec:block" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:block">5</a> chooses the local word and window sizes and proves the overlap estimates. Section <a href="#sec:layout" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:layout">6</a> defines the random observation, proves its projection to a genuine trace, and identifies the law of the hidden bits after the report. Section <a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">7</a> then proves the gain from the forced binary subtree and closes the induction. Section <a href="#sec:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer">8</a> restores genuine traces, pads to every exact length, and proves both main theorems. The extension to arbitrary fixed positive success uses several independently chosen gadgets and posterior factorization.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Distances and channel reduction

For probability laws $P,Q$, define overlap by $\mathop{\mathrm{ov}}(P,Q)=1-\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(P,Q)$, the largest mass of a common subprobability. Our squared Hellinger distance is $$H^2(P,Q)=\int(\sqrt{dP}-\sqrt{dQ})^2.$$ Cauchy–Schwarz and Hellinger affinity give $\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(P,Q)\le H(P,Q)\le\sqrt{2\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(P,Q)}$. Both distances decrease under a common stochastic kernel, and squared Hellinger distance of product laws is at most the sum for the factors. If $Q\ll P$, then $$H^2(P,Q)\le\int\left(\frac{dQ}{dP}-1\right)^2dP.$$ These elementary inequalities also explain why a small likelihood score is an appropriate induction quantity.

For the rest of the proof put $$\delta=\min(q,1/2),\qquad \Lambda=\delta^{-3},\qquad
 H_{\rm del}=\delta^3\log n.$$ We use $\mathrm{Tr}(w)=\mathcal D_\delta(w)$ throughout the construction. The requested channel $q$ is obtained by deleting each surviving bit of a $\delta$-trace with additional probability $(q-\delta)/(1-\delta)$. Thus all upper bounds on distinguishability proved at $\delta$ also hold at $q$. Moreover $$H_{\rm del}\to\infty,\qquad
 \log H_{\rm del}=\log(q^3\log n)+O(1).$$ The working deletion rate $\delta$ remains fixed through every intermediate scale of an instance. In all integer index ranges, endpoints may be real and only integer labels in the range are used.

<a id="sec:scales"></a>
### The induction and global scales

The induction carries three estimates for each pair of deterministic words. Two auxiliary laws $\mu,\nu$ can be postprocessed by one kernel to approximate the genuine trace laws, and the likelihood score $d\nu/d\mu-1$ has a small moment. These two estimates control distinguishability at the current length. The third estimate is common mass between traces of two consecutive powers of the first word; it supplies the hidden orientation bits used to construct the next pair.

Here is the quantitative idea behind the scale choices. Ignoring the small exponent margins, a block of depth $b$ with target length $N$ starts from atoms of length about $N^{2^{-b}}$ whose squared score is at most $N^{-2^{-b}d}$. The low-degree analysis will either suppress a term by signed smoothing or force it to contain $2^b$ old-score factors. Those factors together retain approximately the decay $N^{-d}$. The joint locations of their sibling groups supply the additional decay $N^{-(b-1)/4}$. Sections <a href="#sec:block" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:block">5</a>–<a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">7</a> prove this estimate with the margins used below. They also verify the input strength needed to control high degrees and preserve consecutive-power overlap.

<a id="eq:contraction-exponent"></a>
Fix $0<c<c_+<1/(4\log2)$, then choose a constant integer $B\ge2$ and $0<\zeta<1$ such that $$\frac{(1-\zeta)((B-1)/4-.05)}{B\log2}>c_+>c.$$ Set $$\begin{split}
 D &=\left\lfloor (1-\zeta)\frac{\log H_{\rm del}}{B\log 2}\right\rfloor,
 \qquad j_0=5\cdot 2^B,\qquad
 \epsilon=2^{-4B-100}/(D+1),\\
 b_i &=\begin{cases}2,&1\le i\le j_0,\\ B,&j_0<i\le D.\end{cases}
\end{split}$$ We henceforth take $D>j_0$, as holds eventually. For either allowed block depth $b$, define the contraction exponent $$\label{eq:contraction-exponent}
 a_0(b)=(1+\epsilon)2^{-b}-\epsilon.$$ It is positive for all sufficiently large instances. Define the target scales backwards by $$N_D=n,\qquad N_{i-1}=N_i^{a_0(b_i)}\quad(1\le i\le D),$$ and the score exponents by $$d_0=2.20,\qquad
 d_i=d_{i-1}+(b_i-1)/4-.05\quad(1\le i\le D).$$ Each preliminary depth-two block adds $.20$, so $d_{j_0}=2.20+2^B$. This fixed preliminary phase raises the seed exponent to the strength required by depth-$B$ blocks. The remaining blocks produce the asymptotic gain in the theorem. The nested-overlap proof and the high-degree score estimate will show separately where this stronger input is used.

The remaining parameters control the accumulated projection error and rare events. Put $$\Delta=n^{-100(d_D+B+1)},\qquad
 r_i=2\left\lceil 10^8 2^{10B}(d_D+B+1)
       \frac{\log n}{\log N_i}\right\rceil\quad(0\le i\le D).$$ The even moment orders $r_i$ are nonincreasing with $i$, so a moment bound available at one level is still available at the next. Their dependence on $\log n/\log N_i$ makes the exceptional probabilities small relative to the final-length error budget even at the smallest scale.

<a id="stmt-lem-scale-margins"></a>
<div id="lem:scale-margins" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:smallest-scale"></a>
<a id="eq:uniform-absorption"></a>
**Lemma 3** (Uniform scale margins). *For the parameters above, $$\label{eq:smallest-scale}
 \log N_0\gtrsim_B (\log n)2^{-BD}
 \gtrsim_B (\log n)H_{\rm del}^{-(1-\zeta)}
 =\Lambda H_{\rm del}^{\zeta}.$$ Moreover, $$\epsilon\log N_0\gtrsim_B
 \frac{(\log n)^\zeta}{1+\log\log n}
 \gg\log\log n+\log(d_D+B+1).$$ For every fixed nonnegative integer $h$, uniformly over $0\le i\le D$, $$\label{eq:uniform-absorption}
 (D+1)^h\Lambda=o(\epsilon\log N_0),\qquad
 \exp\bigl(O_B((D+1)^h\Lambda)\bigr)=N_i^{o(\epsilon)}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-scale-margins"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-scale-margins</summary>

*Proof.* For every allowed $b$, $a_0(b)\ge2^{-B}(1-(2^B-1)\epsilon)$. Multiplying these bounds over $D$ blocks loses only a factor bounded below by a positive constant depending on $B$, because $D\epsilon\le2^{-4B-100}$. The definition of $D$ then proves <a href="#eq:smallest-scale" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:smallest-scale">[eq:smallest-scale]</a>. Since $H_{\rm del}\le\log n$ and $D+1=O_B(1+\log\log n)$, the same bound proves the second display. Finally, $$\frac{(D+1)^h\Lambda}{\epsilon\log N_0}
 \lesssim_B\frac{(D+1)^{h+1}}{H_{\rm del}^{\zeta}}
 \longrightarrow0,$$ because $D=O_B(\log H_{\rm del})$. Also $N_i\ge N_0$, proving the uniform exponential formulation. ◻

</details>

In particular, $\log r_i=O_B(D+1)$ uniformly in $i$. Equation <a href="#eq:uniform-absorption" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:uniform-absorption">[eq:uniform-absorption]</a> will control factors involving the channel, the number of blocks, and the fixed-depth calculations. Constants here and below may depend on $B,\zeta,c_+$. Unless additional dependence is explicit, constants in $O$, $\asymp$, $\lesssim$, and similar notation are absolute or depend only on fixed numerical parameters, such as a fixed degree cutoff; they do not depend on $\delta,n,D,r_i$. All estimates used at multiple levels are uniform over those levels as $n\to\infty$. In particular, the working rate $\delta$ always has the value fixed from the original instance, not a value recomputed from $N_i$.

Distinct equal-length words each have an exclusive full-length trace of positive probability, so neither genuine trace law dominates the other. This motivates placing the likelihood score on auxiliary laws and recording their projection errors separately.

<a id="stmt-def-invariant"></a>
<div id="def:invariant" class="definition">

**Definition 4** (The level invariant). Choose $C>2$ and $0<\omega<1/8$ depending only on $\delta$, with $$\log C=O(\Lambda),\qquad \log(\omega^{-1})=O(\Lambda),$$ and a constant $M_B\ge0$ depending only on $B$. The same choices are used at every level. For $0\le i\le D$, a level-$i$ pair consists of two different strings $x_i,y_i$ of the same length $\ell_i\in[N_i/2,N_i]$, and probability laws $\mu_i,\nu_i$ on a common auxiliary space, which may depend on $n$ and $\delta$, satisfying the following conditions.

- There are a Markov kernel $\mathcal K_i$ from the auxiliary space to finite binary words and a number $E_i\ge0$ such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathcal K_i\mu_i,\mathrm{Tr}(x_i))\le E_i,\qquad
   \mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathcal K_i\nu_i,\mathrm{Tr}(y_i))\le E_i,
   \qquad \frac{E_i}{\ell_i}\le(i+1)\Delta.$$

- We have $\nu_i\ll\mu_i$ and $$\left\|\frac{d\nu_i}{d\mu_i}-1\right\|_{L^{r_i}(\mu_i)}^2
         \le N_i^{-d_i}.$$

- For an integer $k_i\in[\ell_i,C^{2Bi+M_B}\ell_i]$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{ov}}\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}(x_i^{k_i}),\mathrm{Tr}(x_i^{k_i+1})\bigr)\ge\omega.$$ Here powers concatenate copies of the word.

</div>

For every positive integer $m$, the first two conditions give $$\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}(x_i)^{\otimes m},\mathrm{Tr}(y_i)^{\otimes m}\bigr)
 \le\sqrt{mN_i^{-d_i}}+2mE_i$$ by Hellinger tensorization and telescoping the projection errors, as detailed in Section <a href="#sec:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer">8</a>. This explains why the squared score carries the sample exponent $d_i$.

The pairs need not hit the target scales exactly. Padding is reserved for the final transfer and is not inserted into words used by later blocks.

<a id="stmt-prop-construction"></a>
<div id="prop:construction" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5** (Construction at every scale). *Fix $B,\zeta,c_+$ and the parameters of this section. Along every sequence with $n\to\infty$ and $\delta^3\log n\to\infty$, for all sufficiently large instances there exist level-$i$ pairs satisfying Definition <a href="#def:invariant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:invariant">4</a> simultaneously for $0\le i\le D$. The choices of $C,\omega$ depend only on $\delta$ and obey the displayed logarithmic bounds, and one $M_B$ depending only on $B$ works at every level.*

</div>

The proof is by induction. Section <a href="#sec:initial" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:initial">4</a> supplies level zero. Proposition <a href="#prop:block" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:block">13</a> constructs the next level, with its observation and likelihood estimates proved in Sections <a href="#sec:layout" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:layout">6</a>–<a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">7</a>. Section <a href="#sec:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer">8</a> applies the final common kernel and returns to complete traces.

<a id="sec:tools"></a>
### Common mass, smoothing, and score moments

The induction needs three operations: converting overlap into hidden offsets, controlling multilinear score expansions, and conditioning on an event where the reference density is bounded below. We prove them with the uniformity needed later.

<a id="stmt-lem-moment"></a>
<div id="lem:moment" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:hc"></a>
**Lemma 6** (Multilinear moment bound). *Let $t\ge2$ be an even integer, let $h\ge0$, and let $(Z_j)_{j\in\mathcal J}$ be a finite family of independent centered real variables with $\|Z_j\|_t\le h$. For an integer $r\ge1$, the degree-$r$ multilinear polynomial with real coefficients $A(S)$ on $r$-subsets satisfies $$\label{eq:hc}
\left\|\sum_{|S|=r} A(S)\prod_{j\in S}Z_j\right\|_t
\le (C_1 t h)^r \|A\|_{\ell^2},$$ where $\ell^2$ uses counting measure and $C_1$ is absolute.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-moment"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-moment</summary>

*Proof.* The tensorization follows the classical principle underlying hypercontractive estimates (Bonami 1970) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Bonami1970](#ref-Bonami1970)]. We give an elementary argument that applies to arbitrary independent centered real coordinates satisfying the stated moment bound; it requires neither symmetry nor a common distribution. Normalize $h=1$ (the zero case is clear). A scalar satisfies $\|b+vZ\|_t^2\le |b|^2+C_2 t |v|^2$ for real $b,v$: if $|v|\le |b|/t$ and $b\ne0$, expand the moment, with the linear term zero, to bound it by $|b|^t(1+O(t^2(v/b)^2))$ using $\sum_{k\ge2}(t|v/b|)^k/k!$. Here concavity gives $(1+O(t^2(v/b)^2))^{2/t}\le
1+O(t(v/b)^2)$. Otherwise use the triangle inequality and $2|bv|\le 2t |v|^2$. Iteration by conditioning on one variable’s complement and applying the triangle inequality in $L^{t/2}$ proves <a href="#eq:hc" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hc">[eq:hc]</a>. We will also apply this to finite families indexed by positions/ranks, identifying ordered increasing tuples with subsets. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-lift"></a>
<div id="lem:lift" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Lifting adjacent-power overlap). *Let $w$ be a nonempty word, $k$ a positive integer, and $0<\omega<1/8$ such that $\mathop{\mathrm{ov}}(\mathrm{Tr}(w^k),\mathrm{Tr}(w^{k+1}))\ge\omega$. There is an absolute constant $C_{\rm abs}$ such that, whenever $C\ge C_{\rm abs}/\omega$, $P$ is a sufficiently large positive integer, and the integer $F$ satisfies $F\ge CkP$, we have <a id="eq:lift"></a>$$\label{eq:lift}
\mathop{\mathrm{ov}}(\mathrm{Tr}(w^{FP}),\mathrm{Tr}(w^{FP+P}))\ge 2/3.$$ It is enough that $P$ exceed an absolute constant, uniformly in $w,k,\omega,C,F$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-lift"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-lift</summary>

*Proof.* This is a quantitative common-part smoothing statement, related in mechanism to consecutive-power smoothing for positive contractions (Zaharopol 1989, Propositions 2.2–2.3) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Zaharopol1989](#ref-Zaharopol1989)]. Write the words as a common tail of $FP-g(k+1/2)$ copies and $g=2\lfloor FP/(2k+1)\rfloor$ chunks of $k$ or $k+1$ copies. Use a uniform random schedule conditional on the total number $m$ of long chunks being $g/2$ or $g/2+P$ respectively. Let $\omega U$ be a common sublaw of chunk trace distributions. Use independent flags of probability $\omega$ to emit the trace under $U$ (hiding the label $k$ or $k+1$), otherwise reveal the label and generate from the normalized residual for that label. The augmented laws postprocess to the claimed ordinary ones by joining the chunks and simulating the known tail. Given the flags and the sequence of visible labels, the other data have the same conditional law on both hypotheses. The number $M$ of hidden flags belongs to $[\omega g/2,2\omega g]$ except with probability at most $2\exp(-c'\omega g)$ for an absolute $c'>0$ by the multiplicative binomial Chernoff bound, where $\omega g\ge c'' \omega C P^2$ for an absolute $c''>0$ at all large $P$. Conditional on such flags, the total variation between visible-label sequences equals that of visible long counts $v$ since the sequences are exchangeable on the visible indices. For total $m$ this count has hypergeometric law $p_m$, with $$p_m(v)={\binom{g-M}{v}\binom{M}{m-v}}/{\binom{g}{m}}.$$ Uniformly for $g/2\le m\le g/2+P$, $\sup_v p_m(v)=O((\omega g)^{-1/2})$. To see this, consecutive probability ratios locate a mode within $O(1)$ of $(g-M)m/g$, e.g. a mode is $\lfloor(g-M+1)(m+1)/(g+2)\rfloor$. For large $P$ the counts and complements in the numerator at the mode are all fixed positive fractions (say between $1/4$ and $3/4$) of the respective trial totals because $g\gtrsim C P^2$, $M\ge\omega g/2\gtrsim P^2$ and $g-M\ge (1-2\omega)g$. Stirling then gives the bound (the exponential entropy factor is at most one by concavity). Couple adjacent $m,m+1$ by uniformly flipping one short label. This increases $v$ by one with probability $t(v)=(g-M-v)/(g-m)$ conditional on $v$. Hence $p_{m+1}(v)-p_m(v)=t(v-1)p_m(v-1)-t(v)p_m(v)$. The sequence $t(v)p_m(v)$ on the support, extended by zero, is unimodal by log-concavity. Thus the adjacent total variation is $\max_v t(v)p_m(v)$, bounded by $\max_v p_m(v)$. The full variation cost of all $P$ steps is $O(P/\sqrt{\omega g})$ plus the rare flag probability, at most $1/3$ at large $P$ by choosing $C\omega$ greater than a sufficiently large absolute constant. Taking smaller common sublaws when necessary lets us use exactly mass $\omega$ in the flag construction. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Paired hiding and the reported information

The paired construction below uses a joint common component of mass $\omega^2$; on this component, the orientation remains fair conditional on the flag and both reported side outputs. Common-part constructions appear in the average-case block argument of McGregor, Price, and Vorotnikova (2014, sec. 3.3) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[MPV2014](#ref-MPV2014)] and the partial-trace construction in the proof of Holden and Lyons (2020, Lemma 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[HoldenLyons2020](#ref-HoldenLyons2020)].

Figure <a href="#fig:paired-hiding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:paired-hiding">1</a> depicts one pair and the resulting uncertainty in the number of copies to the left of the central segment.

<figure id="fig:paired-hiding">

<figcaption>Let $ωU$ be a common sublaw of the trace laws of $w^{k}$ and $w^{k + 1}$. For orientation $E$, $P_{E}$ is the product of its two side laws and $R_{E}$ is the normalized joint residual. Across $J$ independent pairs straddling the segment, $M ∼ Bin(J,ω^{2})$ before reporting. The lower panel conditions on flags, side outputs, and visible bits; $L_{\rm left}$ counts only copies contributed by the left pair members. Other fixed material adds a deterministic location offset. Block widths are schematic.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-paired-hiding"></a>

     \(L_{\rm left}:=Jk+\sum_{i=1}^{J}E_i\) copies of \(w\) on the left.\\[3pt]

     and the hidden count \(M\) are fixed, and\\[3pt]

Visual witness description of PDF page 9 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

  - Two rows representing configurations for orientation $E$:
    - Row "$E = 0$": three contiguous rectangular blocks labeled "$w^k$", "central segment", and "$w^{k+1}$".
    - Row "$E = 1$": three contiguous rectangular blocks labeled "$w^{k+1}$", "central segment", and "$w^k$".
  - A downward bracket/arrow leads to a wide rounded rectangle containing the formula:
    $$P_E = \omega^2(U \otimes U) + (1 - \omega^2)R_E$$
  - Two downward arrows branch from this formula into two side-by-side rectangular boxes:
    - Left box: "Hidden: $E$ is not reported." and "Emit $U \otimes U$; $E$ remains fair."
    - Right box: "Visible: report $E$." and "Emit the joint residual $R_E$."

    - Text: "$J$ pairs contribute $L_{\text{left}} := Jk + \sum_{i=1}^J E_i$ copies of $w$ on the left."

Throughout, unless window boundaries or additional payloads are specified explicitly, report only one unseparated output per segment/chunk/residual, plus flags and revealed orientation bits as indicated (not original word boundaries internal to that output). Given a word $w$ and a count $k$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{ov}}(\mathrm{Tr}(w^k),\mathrm{Tr}(w^{k+1}))\ge\omega$, choose a common sublaw $\omega U$. Two chunks use a paired random bit orientation giving lengths $(k,k+1)$ or $(k+1,k)$ even when far separated in the concatenation. Use one joint flag of probability $\omega^2$ and output from $U\otimes U$ on it, otherwise reveal the bit and generate the two traces jointly from the normalized residual after subtracting $\omega^2 U\otimes U$ from the product law for that orientation. Marginalizing flags yields the requisite conditionally independent pair traces given orientations. Conditional on the augmented pair data, the bit remains fair on the hidden event. Independent pairs thus carry independent hidden fair bits conditional on this part of the report. All auxiliary schedules/flags are redrawn independently for each trace.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Conditioning to obtain a small likelihood score

Separating a typical region from a small exceptional mass is also used in Hellinger estimates for trace reconstruction (Chase 2021a, arXiv v2, Section 2) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Chase2021Lower](#ref-Chase2021Lower)]. The following version keeps the full moment and simulation-error budgets.

<a id="stmt-lem-clipping"></a>
<div id="lem:clipping" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:clipcond"></a>
**Lemma 8** (Clipping with a common event). *Fix a level $0\le i\le D$ and its parameters from Section <a href="#sec:scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:scales">2</a>. Suppose preliminary oracle laws have common “side” metadata with law $Q_{\rm side}$, and conditional densities $G_x,G_y$ over a conditional reference $Q$, possibly depending on metadata. Require $\int G_x\,dQ=\int G_y\,dQ=1$ for $Q_{\rm side}$-almost every metadata value. Use $Q_{\rm side}Q$ as the joint reference. Suppose on a typical metadata event of probability $\ge1-o(\Delta)$ we have uniform estimates $$\label{eq:clipcond}
\|G_x-1\|_{r_i},\|G_y-1\|_{r_i}\le N_i^{-\rho},
\qquad \|G_y-G_x\|_{r_i}^2\le N_i^{-d_i-.01}$$ in $L^{r_i}(Q)$, where $\rho\ge 2^{-4B-10}$. Then conditioning each law on the event consisting of typical metadata and $G_x\ge1/2$ changes it in total variation by $o(\Delta)$. The conditioned laws $\mu,\nu$ satisfy $\nu\ll\mu$ and $$\|d\nu/d\mu-1\|_{L^{r_i}(\mu)}^2\le N_i^{-d_i}.$$ All these conclusions hold uniformly over $0\le i\le D$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-clipping"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-clipping</summary>

*Proof.* Condition each preliminary law separately on the same joint event $A_{\mathrm{keep}}$ consisting of typical metadata and $G_x\ge1/2$. Its complement has joint reference measure on the typical part $\le(2N_i^{-\rho})^{r_i}$, hence bad mass $o(\Delta)$ under each preliminary law by Cauchy–Schwarz, the moment bound, and the common atypical mass. The conditioned laws therefore change postprocessing by $o(\Delta)$ in total variation. Because $G_x\ge1/2$ on the conditioning event $A_{\mathrm{keep}}$, the new $y$-law is dominated by the new $x$-law and their likelihood ratio on the latter support is $c' G_y/G_x$ with a scalar $c'=1+o(\Delta)$. Also $$\int_{\{G_x\ge1/2,\ \mathrm{typical}\}}
  \left|G_y/G_x-1\right|^{r_i} G_x\,d(Q_{\rm side}Q)
 \le 2^{r_i-1} N_i^{-(d_i+.01)r_i/2}.$$ Under the conditioned $x$-law this yields $\|G_y/G_x-1\|_{r_i}\le 3 N_i^{-(d_i+.01)/2}$ for all large $n$ by taking the $r_i$-th root (the conditioning probability is $1-o(\Delta)$). Thus $\|c'G_y/G_x-1\|_{r_i}\le
c' \|G_y/G_x-1\|_{r_i}+|c'-1|\le N_i^{-d_i/2}$ as desired for large $n$, since $\Delta\le N_i^{-100 d_D}$. In this calculation and throughout, any $o(\Delta)$ masses claimed from <a href="#eq:clipcond" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:clipcond">[eq:clipcond]</a> follow uniformly using the definition of $r_i$ and $\log N_i\to\infty$; no uniform lower bound on the unconditioned ratio or density is needed. For Hellinger comparisons of preliminary laws one can similarly use the squared density difference times $2$ on $G_x\ge1/2$ and $G_x+G_y$ on its complement. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:initial"></a>
### An initial pair at the working deletion rate

This section supplies the induction with a pair whose squared score decays as $N_0^{-2.20}$ and whose repeated traces have enough common mass to hide later offsets. The initial words have one defect in each alternating period. A finite signed pattern moves some of these defects by one slot in opposite directions. We first describe an observation that forgets the original slot labels, then use its conditional rank probabilities for two purposes: obtaining common mass for pure runs and bounding the score of the signed pair.

<a id="stmt-prop-initial"></a>
<div id="prop:initial" class="proposition">

**Proposition 9** (Initial pair). *For the parameters of Section <a href="#sec:scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:scales">2</a>, there is a choice of the common constants in Definition <a href="#def:invariant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:invariant">4</a> with $M_B=1$ for which a level-zero pair exists with simulation error $E_0\le\Delta$. One may choose $\omega=\exp(-C_9\Lambda)$ for an absolute constant $C_9$, and $C>2$ with $\log C=O(\Lambda)$.*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Words and the reported mark lists

In this section $C_*,c_*>0$ denote absolute constants whose values may change between estimates; constants indexed by $r$ or $s_0$ may depend on those fixed indices.

Put $N=N_0$, $\eta=.004$, and $$m=\lfloor N^{1/2-\eta}\rfloor,\qquad
 K=\lfloor N^{1/2-2\eta}\rfloor,\qquad
 k=\lfloor\delta m\rfloor,$$ $$J=\left\lfloor
       \frac{\lfloor N/(2m-1)\rfloor-K}{2k+1}
    \right\rfloor,\qquad
 P=J(2k+1)+K.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:scale-margins" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:scale-margins">3</a> gives $\Lambda=o(\log N)$, hence $\delta^{-1}=N^{o(1)}$. In particular $\delta^3m\to\infty$, $m\delta^2\to\infty$, and $K,k+1\le m$ for all sufficiently large instances. The definitions then give $$J\asymp\delta^{-1}N^{2\eta},\qquad J=o(K),\qquad
 P(2m-1)\in[N/2,N].$$ For the last assertion, $P$ differs from $\lfloor N/(2m-1)\rfloor$ by fewer than $2k+1$, so the omitted length is $O(km+m)=o(N)$.

For this construction only, let the ordinary block be $\mathsf A=01$ and the defect block be $\mathsf D=1$. For $b\in\{-1,0,1\}$, define the period $$W_b=\mathsf A^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor+b-1}\,
       \mathsf D\,
       \mathsf A^{m-\lfloor m/2\rfloor-b}.$$ It has $m$ blocks, with the defect at block position $\lfloor m/2\rfloor+b$, and length $2m-1$. Period, slot, and rank indices in this section start at $1$. For even $m$, the adjacent types $W_0,W_1$ are exactly the shifted-defect pair displayed by Chase (2021a, arXiv v2, Introduction and Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Chase2021Lower](#ref-Chase2021Lower)]; for odd $m$, they are the corresponding smaller pair with one common final $\mathsf A$ appended. Below we compare $W_{-1}$ with $W_1$ in exceptional periods and make signed choices across many periods.

Set $$\begin{gathered}
 a=\lfloor P/2\rfloor,\qquad s_0=300,\qquad I=\{0,\ldots,2^{s_0}-1\},\\
 \sum_{i\in I}\beta_i z^i=\prod_{v=0}^{s_0-1}(1-z^{2^v}).
\end{gathered}$$ The two words $x_0,y_0$ have $P$ periods. Every period is $W_0$ except those at $a+i$, $i\in I$, where $x_0$ uses $W_{\beta_i}$ and $y_0$ uses $W_{-\beta_i}$. For all sufficiently large instances these indices lie in $\{1,\ldots,P\}$. Both words have length $\ell_0=P(2m-1)\in[N/2,N]$, and they differ: the parse into $01$ and $1$ from the left is unique. The signs are the classical Prouhet–Thue–Morse signs; their polynomial has a zero of order $s_0$ at $1$ (Allouche and Shallit 1999, Proposition 2 and Theorem 6) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[AlloucheShallit1999](#ref-AlloucheShallit1999)].

We now define the more informative observation used only at this level. For $H\in\{\mathsf A,\mathsf D\}$, put $$r_e=\delta^2/2,\qquad
 \nu_H=\frac{\mathrm{Tr}(H)-r_e\delta_\varnothing}{1-r_e},$$ where $\varnothing$ denotes the empty string. This reserves the same empty output mass in both block laws. In the proof of Lemma 2.1, steps (I) and (III), Holden and Lyons (2020) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[HoldenLyons2020](#ref-HoldenLyons2020)] first erase a mass $q^2/2$ from $01$ blocks at their deletion rate $q$; here the same decomposition is applied at rate $\delta$ to both $01$ and $1$, followed by an explicit likelihood score.

For each block independently, silently erase it with probability $r_e$; otherwise report a mark with law $\nu_H$. The mark alphabet is $$\mathcal M_{\rm mark}=\{\varnothing,0,1,01\}.$$ An observation on a sequence of blocks is the ordered list of its returning marks, an element of $\bigsqcup_{l\ge0}\mathcal M_{\rm mark}^l$. The list reports its individual entries, including empty-valued entries, but no original slot labels or period boundaries. Thus the empty list and the one-entry list $(\varnothing)$ are distinct. Its count $l$ is the number of entries, not the number of bits in their concatenation. Concatenating the mark strings gives the ordinary trace law, since $\mathrm{Tr}(H)=r_e\delta_\varnothing+(1-r_e)\nu_H$.

Under $\nu_{\mathsf A}$, define $$Z(Y)=\frac{\nu_{\mathsf D}(Y)}{\nu_{\mathsf A}(Y)}-1.$$ The defect law is dominated by the ordinary-block law. Its support is contained in $\{\varnothing,1\}$, where the likelihood ratios are $$\frac{\delta-\delta^2/2}{\delta^2/2}
 \quad\text{and}\quad \frac1\delta,$$ respectively. Hence $Z$ is centered, $\|Z\|_\infty=O(\delta^{-1})$, and $\|Z\|_{L^2(\nu_{\mathsf A})}^2\lesssim\delta^{-1}$, uniformly for $\delta\le1/2$.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Conditional densities and the rank kernel

Consider $1\le u\le m$ periods with fixed types $\mathbf b=(b_1,\ldots,b_u)\in\{-1,0,1\}^u$. There are $n_b=um$ slots, and the defect in period $j$ is at $$p_{\mathbf b}(j)=(j-1)m+\lfloor m/2\rfloor+b_j.$$ The return count has law $\operatorname{Bin}(n_b,1-r_e)$, independent of $\mathbf b$. Conditional on any count $0\le l\le n_b$, the returning subset $S_l$ is uniform among the size-$l$ subsets of $\{1,\ldots,n_b\}$. This subset is not reported. If $p\in S_l$, let $\operatorname{rk}_{S_l}(p)$ be its rank in the increasing order on $S_l$. Put $Q_l=\nu_{\mathsf A}^{\otimes l}$, with $Q_0$ the point mass on the empty list.

<a id="eq:base-density"></a>
<a id="eq:base-density-expansion"></a>
Relative to $Q_l$, the density of the reported list $Y=(Y_1,\ldots,Y_l)$ is $$\label{eq:base-density}
 G_{\mathbf b}^{(u,l)}(Y)
 =\mathbb E_{S_l}
   \prod_{\{j:p_{\mathbf b}(j)\in S_l\}}
       \bigl(1+Z(Y_{\operatorname{rk}_{S_l}(p_{\mathbf b}(j))})\bigr).$$ Empty products are $1$, so this also defines the density at $l=0$. It integrates to one because each $1+Z$ has mean one under $\nu_{\mathsf A}$. To expand it, for $1\le r\le u$ take an increasing tagged tuple $\mathbf j=(j_1,\ldots,j_r)$ of periods and an increasing rank tuple $1\le v_1<\cdots<v_r\le l$. Write $p_h=p_{\mathbf b}(j_h)$, and put <a id="eq:base-rank-event"></a>$$\label{eq:base-rank-event}
\begin{split}
 W_{\mathbf j,\mathbf b}(v)
 &=\mathbb P\bigl\{
 p_h\in S_l,\ 
 \operatorname{rk}_{S_l}(p_h)=v_h
 \text{ for every }h
 \bigr\},\\
 A_{r,\mathbf b}(v)&=\sum_{1\le j_1<\cdots<j_r\le u}
                         W_{\mathbf j,\mathbf b}(v).
\end{split}$$ The event in $W_{\mathbf j,\mathbf b}$ imposes no restriction on untagged slots, including other defects. It is the joint probability of the tagged returns and ranks within the count conditioning, rather than a rank law conditioned again on all tags returning. Expanding the product in <a href="#eq:base-density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:base-density">[eq:base-density]</a> now gives $$\label{eq:base-density-expansion}
 G_{\mathbf b}^{(u,l)}(Y)
 =1+\sum_{r=1}^{u}\ \sum_{1\le v_1<\cdots<v_r\le l}
       A_{r,\mathbf b}(v)\prod_{h=1}^r Z(Y_{v_h}).$$

<a id="eq:base-rank-kernel"></a>
Define the slot gaps and rank gaps by $$\begin{aligned}
 (g_0,\ldots,g_r)
   &=(p_1-1,p_2-p_1-1,\ldots,p_r-p_{r-1}-1,n_b-p_r),\\
 (z_0,\ldots,z_r)
   &=(v_1-1,v_2-v_1-1,\ldots,v_r-v_{r-1}-1,l-v_r).
\end{aligned}$$ Their sums are $n_b-r$ and $l-r$. Put $\theta=l/n_b$ and $f_g(z)=\mathbb P\{\operatorname{Bin}(g,\theta)=z\}$. Counting returning untagged slots in each gap gives the exact kernel $$\label{eq:base-rank-kernel}
\begin{aligned}
 W_{\mathbf j,\mathbf b}(v)
 &=\frac{\prod_{i=0}^{r}\binom{g_i}{z_i}}{\binom{n_b}{l}}\\
 &=\frac{\theta^r}{f_{n_b}(l)}
       \prod_{i=0}^{r}f_{g_i}(z_i).
